            "Those corrections sharpen the design considerably. I overstated both the novelty of the worldtube degrees of freedom and the scope of Code QC.\n\n## Reframing the worldline-to-worldtube transition\n\nThe transition is not:\n\n\\[\n\\text{zero-thickness curve}\\rightarrow\\text{object with thickness and bending}.\n\\]\n\nSAT has already treated the filament as finite, bendable, twistable, tension-bearing, and interaction-capable. The transition is closer to:\n\n\\[\n\\text{centerline-essentialist description}\n\\rightarrow\n\\text{explicit finite-core boundary and intersection description}.\n\\]\n\nThe worldline model has largely encoded the object through the evolution of its axial history. H(s)H now needs to make explicit whatever was formerly carried implicitly by:\n\n- effective thickness;\n- boundary behavior;\n- resistance to penetration or overlap;\n- rotational state;\n- coupling at contact;\n- interaction with \\(R_\\Sigma\\);\n- morphology of the resolved intersection.\n\nThe number of modeled physical degrees of freedom may not increase dramatically. Some formerly implicit variables simply become explicit and properly located.\n\n## ER-bridge accessibility\n\nIf the filament is represented as an ER bridge, an exterior observer need not have access to a literal interior cross-section. The accessible interface may consist only of:\n\n- effective thickness;\n- boundary location;\n- boundary response;\n- interaction range;\n- penetration/exclusion behavior;\n- rotation and circulation;\n- induced phase or holonomy;\n- the geometry of its resolved intersection.\n\nSo “cross-sectional geometry” should not automatically mean an ordinary material disk being carried along a curve. We need to distinguish:\n\n1. Unresolved bridge interior.\n2. Effective external boundary.\n3. Boundary dynamics.\n4. \\(R_\\Sigma\\)-intersection geometry.\n5. Observable interaction properties.\n\nThat gives the Kerr/ergosphere, Schwarzschild-like, event-horizon-like, and naked-ring constructions a cleaner place. They are candidate effective boundary/intersection models—not necessarily claims about a visible interior.\n\nLikewise, there are several progressively stronger statements hiding inside “the intersection is a closed string”:\n\n1. The intersection is a closed loop.\n2. Its allowable deformations resemble closed-string modes.\n3. Its mode spectrum maps to a particular string formalism.\n4. The imported string dynamics reproduce the relevant particle behavior.\n5. The string formulation becomes an equivalent readout of H(s)H.\n\nThe first might follow geometrically. The later steps need separate evaluation. The supervisor must prevent them from collapsing into one identification.\n\nThe alternative boundary candidates should remain explicitly parallel:\n\n- Kerr/ergosphere-like boundary;\n- Schwarzschild-like oscillatory boundary;\n- naked-ring model;\n- regularized ring or finite-core analogue;\n- some construction not yet represented.\n\nThey should be compared by discriminants such as loop topology, rotation/chirality, boundary permeability, mode spectrum, regularity, limiting behavior, and observable interaction properties.\n\n## Lean and a model adjudicator\n\nYour proposed role for Lean is stronger and more interesting than merely checking completed derivations.\n\nA mature combined system could be:\n\n- UI generates or reconstructs admissible geometric configurations.\n- Whirligig navigates transformations or candidate derivation paths.\n- Python explores numerical behavior.\n- Lean checks that the transformations, exclusions, invariants, and conclusions follow from the declared model grammar.\n\nThat could become a genuine model adjudicator in this restricted sense:\n\n> Given the frozen representational commitments and admissibility rules, determine which constructions are allowed, which are equivalent, and which violate the formal system.\n\nIt still would not establish that the grammar is physically adequate. But it could do much more than check isolated algebra. It could enforce the model’s internal court of appeal.\n\nThe critical distinction is:\n\n\\[\n\\text{Lean-verified within model}\n\\neq\n\\text{empirically supported},\n\\]\n\nwhile also recognizing:\n\n\\[\n\\text{internally inconsistent}\n\\Rightarrow\n\\text{inadmissible regardless of data match}.\n\\]\n\nCODATA conformity becomes meaningful evidence only after internal consistency, traceability, and honest parameter accounting have passed. A match cannot rescue a broken derivation. At best, valid matches indicate that the representation may be approaching observational isometry.\n\n## Strict speculative and formal silos\n\nI think we need three lanes rather than one continuous ladder.\n\n| Lane | Purpose | Allowed outputs |\n|---|---|---|\n| Speculative | Explore possibilities, metaphors, candidate mappings, alternative geometries | Hypothesis records and proposed tests |\n| Formal candidate | Specify equations, assumptions, types, reductions, code, and formal statements | Testable modules |\n| Backbone | Hold only approved, integrated model choices | Active SAT/H(s)H mathematics |\n\nA speculative item cannot directly modify the backbone.\n\nPromotion into the formal lane requires:\n\n- a clearly stated target gap;\n- an exact object definition;\n- a source or construction trail;\n- explicit assumptions;\n- a proposed discriminant;\n- approval to spend formalization effort.\n\nPromotion into the backbone requires the later mathematical, computational, empirical, cross-sector, and integration gates.\n\nThis allows Python and Lean to test speculation without embalming it. The formal lane is a laboratory bench, not a mausoleum.\n\n## Correct separation of Code QC and later testing\n\nYou are right: I blurred stages that need to stay cleanly separate.\n\n### Stage 1: Pre-run Code QC\n\nNotebookLM or another code-review LLM checks only:\n\n- Does the code implement the supplied equations?\n- Are variables mapped correctly?\n- Are signs, factors, indices, and operators preserved?\n- Are initial and boundary conditions represented?\n- Are undocumented clamps, substitutions, normalizations, or target values present?\n- Does every routine trace back to the specification?\n\nIt does not assess output because no output exists yet.\n\n### Stage 2: Execution qualification\n\nYou or a runnable ChatGPT instance executes the code.\n\nThe code itself should include a behavioral test matrix:\n\n| Input class | Required behavior |\n|---|---|\n| Known-valid parameters | Run successfully and satisfy declared invariants |\n| Dummy/incompatible parameters | Fail in a specified, recognizable manner |\n| Missing parameters | Refuse execution with a precise diagnostic |\n| Near-valid parameters | Produce predictable warnings or controlled deviations |\n| Boundary parameters | Exhibit the expected transition or declared loss of validity |\n| Perturbed parameters | Respond according to stated symmetry, continuity, or monotonicity expectations |\n\nThe goal is not merely “the program runs.” It is:\n\n> We know in advance which classes of input should succeed, fail, warn, or change regime—and the implementation recognizes them.\n\n### Stage 3: Numerical behavior evaluation\n\nOnly after execution qualification:\n\n- convergence;\n- conditioning;\n- tolerance dependence;\n- sensitivity;\n- conservation;\n- discretization effects;\n- solver dependence;\n- initial-condition dependence;\n- repeatability.\n\n### Stage 4: Model evaluation\n\nOnly here do we assess:\n\n- CODATA conformity;\n- control/ablation results;\n- holdouts;\n- cross-sector consequences;\n- parameter economy;\n- whether the right result arose for the right structural reason.\n\n### Stage 5: Integration and scientific review\n\nThen:\n\n- Does it fit the intended scope?\n- Does it contradict another module?\n- Is there a simpler formalism?\n- Is an imported framework doing unnecessary work?\n- Is the explanation legible to mainstream physicists?\n- Can the same content be expressed using more standard mathematical language?\n- Does it clarify the model or merely add machinery?\n\nThese are separate reports and should never be compressed into one “QC passed.”\n\n## Symbol management\n\nYour namespace intuition is right. The key is to separate mathematical notation from symbol identity.\n\nI would use three coordinated layers.\n\n### 1. Immutable symbol identity\n\nEvery imported symbol receives a machine-safe registry ID:\n\n```text\nSYM-BV-G-0001\nSYM-GR-G-0001\nSYM-PDE-G-0001\n```\n\nThis is the actual identity used for tracking. It never changes, even if the displayed notation later does.\n\n### 2. Human-facing provenance notation\n\nDuring the compilation period:\n\n\\[\nG\\llbracket \\mathrm{GR}\\rrbracket,\\qquad\nG\\llbracket \\mathrm{PDE}\\rrbracket,\\qquad\nn\\llbracket \\mathrm{BV}\\rrbracket.\n\\]\n\nI prefer a provenance tag such as `⟦GR⟧` over an ordinary superscript or subscript because superscripts and subscripts already carry mathematical meaning. A source tag should not be mistaken for a power, tensor index, component, or scale label.\n\nIn plaintext:\n\n```text\nG⟦GR⟧\nG⟦PDE⟧\nn⟦BV⟧\n```\n\nIn LaTeX, a macro could render it consistently:\n\n```latex\n\\srcsym{GR}{G}\n\\srcsym{PDE}{G}\n\\srcsym{BV}{n}\n```\n\nThe macro’s appearance can change later without rewriting every equation.\n\n### 3. Runtime notation\n\nPython:\n\n```python\ngr__G\npde__G\nbv__n\n```\n\nLean:\n\n```lean\nGR.G\nPDE.G\nBV.n\n```\n\nThis avoids depending on exotic Unicode support in code, search, normalization, fonts, terminals, or theorem libraries. Decorative Unicode can still be used in visual output, but it should not be the only carrier of identity. A magnificent Glagolitic \\(G\\) is delightful until one font silently turns it into a bureaucratic coup.\n\n### Symbol registry fields\n\nEach entry should record:\n\n- immutable symbol ID;\n- displayed native symbol;\n- formalism namespace;\n- exact source;\n- native definition;\n- semantic role;\n- mathematical type;\n- domain and codomain;\n- physical dimensions/units;\n- index conventions;\n- assumptions;\n- aliases;\n- code name;\n- Lean name;\n- current SAT mapping;\n- maturity status;\n- first and last use;\n- superseded-by relationship.\n\nCanonicalization then becomes an explicit operation:\n\n```text\nSYM-BV-G-0001\n    --mapped-by MAP-SYM-004-->\nSYM-SAT-G-0003\n```\n\nNothing silently “becomes \\(G\\).”\n\n## Formalism namespace versus documentary source\n\nThese should also remain distinct.\n\nFor example:\n\n- `BV` identifies the Batalin–Vilkovisky formalism.\n- `SRC-0142` identifies the exact paper or source document.\n- `EXT-BV-0017` identifies our extraction from that source.\n- `MAP-WT-0006` identifies the proposed worldtube adaptation.\n\nA paper is not the formalism, and an LLM’s extraction is not the paper.\n\n## Path-chain encoding\n\nEvery important result should be expressible as a typed path:\n\n```text\nSRC-0142\n  → EXT-BV-0017\n  → MAP-WT-0006\n  → EQ-WT-0023\n  → SPEC-PY-0011\n  → CODE-PY-0048\n  → RUN-0092\n  → EVAL-0031\n  → AUD-0014\n  → MOD-WT-0004\n```\n\nEvery arrow also needs a type:\n\n- `quotes`\n- `extracts`\n- `specializes`\n- `translates`\n- `defines`\n- `derives`\n- `implements`\n- `executes`\n- `tests`\n- `audits`\n- `supersedes`\n- `integrates`\n\nThe supervisor’s most useful diagnostic may simply be:\n\n> Show me the shortest complete path from this claimed result back to its empirical and mathematical inputs.\n\nA missing edge is visible immediately.\n\n## Keeping the repository efficient\n\nThe skill and repository should remain separate:\n\n- Skill: stable laboratory protocol and supervisory behavior.\n- Repository: changing sources, symbols, equations, modules, code, reports, and decisions.\n\nA lean initial repository might be:\n\n```text\nsat-hsh/\n├── control/\n│   ├── object-hierarchy.yaml\n│   ├── namespaces.yaml\n│   ├── symbols.yaml\n│   ├── statuses.yaml\n│   └── labs.yaml\n├── registry/\n│   ├── sources.yaml\n│   ├── equations.yaml\n│   ├── claims.yaml\n│   └── modules.yaml\n├── chains/\n├── speculative/\n├── modules/\n├── experiments/\n├── formal/\n├── reports/\n└── publication/\n```\n\nEfficiency rule:\n\n- A speculative thought gets one registry record, not a directory tree.\n- A formal candidate gets a module directory only after promotion.\n- Code and Lean live with the module they test.\n- Reports are generated from module metadata rather than rewritten manually.\n- The plaintext derivation is normative.\n- LaTeX is a rendered companion.\n- Publication manuscripts consume frozen modules rather than becoming the only place the derivations exist.\n\n## Revised LaTeXer role\n\nAgreed: LaTeXer is primarily responsible for:\n\n- derivation-module typesetting;\n- equations;\n- explanatory background;\n- diagrams and tables where helpful;\n- supplementary rendered versions of canonical plaintext;\n- later assembly into public theory archives and papers.\n\nIt does not own the derivation, status, or canonical wording. The plaintext module and registry do.\n\n## What belongs in the eventual supervisory skill\n\nAfter these corrections, its stable contents should be:\n\n1. Laboratory roles and routing.\n2. Speculative/formal/backbone silo rules.\n3. Assignment and return-packet protocols.\n4. Symbol namespace and registry rules.\n5. Typed path-chain requirements.\n6. Promotion and rejection gates.\n7. Exact limits of pre-run Code QC.\n8. Runtime test-contract requirements.\n9. Derivation-verification and Lean-routing rules.\n10. CODATA/holdout/cross-sector evidence rules.\n11. Integration, scope, economy, and sanity review.\n12. Mainstream-physics translation review.\n13. Self-audit and source-access honesty.\n14. Nathan as final authority for promotion to an SAT model choice.\n\nThe dynamic equations, current modules, lab assignments, and project status should not live inside the skill. They belong in the repository and living control ledger.\n\nThe next design object should probably be the typed path-chain schema, because it determines what every lab must return, how symbols remain traceable, and how the repository stays navigable. The symbol registry can then be designed as one of the chain’s core node types."

          "parts": [
            "Yes. That makes the path chain fundamentally an agent-signature lineage, with mathematical and computational artifacts attached to it.\n\nThe system needs two new registries:\n\n1. Agent Registry — who each LLM instance is, its role, rules, corpus, and setup version.\n2. Capability Registry — what that particular instance can actually do, with what degree of reliability.\n\n## Signature grammar\n\nYour proposed form works:\n\n```text\n[PySpc.260721.0095]\n```\n\nI would define it as:\n\n```text\n[AGENT.YYMMDD.SEQUENCE]\n```\n\nWhere:\n\n- `AGENT` is the unique registered abbreviation for one specific LLM instance.\n- `YYMMDD` is the response date in the project’s declared timezone.\n- `SEQUENCE` is that agent’s monotonically increasing reply number, padded to at least four digits.\n- Sequence numbers are never reused.\n\nI recommend that the sequence not reset daily. The date tells us when the response occurred; the global per-agent sequence prevents collisions among parallel chats or late-night date transitions.\n\nThus:\n\n```text\n[PySpc.260721.0095]\n[PySpc.260721.0096]\n[PySpc.260722.0097]\n```\n\nEach reply inside that LLM’s own conversation gets its individual tag.\n\n## Internal reply versus signatory reply\n\nWe should distinguish ordinary internal responses from responses formally handed downstream.\n\nEvery response is tagged:\n\n```text\n[PySpc.260721.0095]\n```\n\nBut only a designated signatory response enters the global lineage.\n\nExample internal sequence:\n\n```text\n[PySpc.260721.0093] Draft implementation\n[PySpc.260721.0094] Corrected boundary condition\n[PySpc.260721.0095] Final handoff packet\n```\n\nOnly `.0095` appears in the handoff chain. Otherwise a long exploratory conversation would produce a lineage hundreds of segments long.\n\nThe signatory packet would say:\n\n```text\nRESPONSE: [PySpc.260721.0095]\nSIGNATORY_ACTION: IMPLEMENTED\nLINEAGE: [MxEq.260719.0027-PySpc.260721.0095]\n```\n\nThe complete example then becomes:\n\n```text\n[PySpc.260721.0095-EqC.260723.0004-Ev.260723.0133]\n```\n\nMeaning:\n\n1. `PySpc.260721.0095` supplied the signed implementation.\n2. `EqC.260723.0004` performed its declared equation-to-code check.\n3. `Ev.260723.0133` performed its declared evaluation.\n\n## A signature records custody, not correctness\n\nThis distinction needs to be explicit. The chain alone says only that an agent performed some operation on the work.\n\nEach signature must carry an action:\n\n- `EXTRACTED`\n- `TRANSLATED`\n- `SPECIFIED`\n- `IMPLEMENTED`\n- `CODE_QC`\n- `EXECUTED`\n- `EVALUATED`\n- `FORMALIZED`\n- `INTEGRATED`\n- `APPROVED`\n- `FORMATTED`\n- `REJECTED`\n- `QUARANTINED`\n\nTherefore:\n\n```text\nEqC.260723.0004\n```\n\ndoes not imply that the code ran or produced valid results. Its setup file defines `EqC` as pre-run equation-to-code QC, and its action records precisely what it checked.\n\nLikewise, a LaTeXer signature means the material was typeset—not that the derivation was independently validated.\n\n## Branches and merges\n\nA linear string works for sequential handoffs, but parallel evaluation produces a directed graph.\n\nSuppose the Python output goes independently to Code QC and a mathematical auditor:\n\n```text\n[PySpc.260721.0095-EqC.260723.0004]\n[PySpc.260721.0095-MthA.260723.0018]\n```\n\nWhen an integrator combines them, we should not fabricate a single order between `EqC` and `MthA`. The integration packet should use multiple parents:\n\n```text\nRESPONSE: [Intg.260724.0007]\nSIGNATORY_ACTION: INTEGRATED\n\nPARENT_LINEAGES:\n  - [PySpc.260721.0095-EqC.260723.0004]\n  - [PySpc.260721.0095-MthA.260723.0018]\n```\n\nThe repository stores the canonical graph. The bracketed chain is the convenient human-readable path through that graph.\n\nFor very long chains, we can assign a chain ID:\n\n```text\nCHAIN_ID: CHN.260721.0017\n```\n\nThe full lineage remains in the ledger; filenames and reports can use the compact chain ID.\n\n## Responses and artifacts need different identities\n\nOne response may create several equations, code files, or reports. Therefore, agent signatures should not double as artifact identifiers.\n\nExample:\n\n```text\nRESPONSE: [PySpc.260721.0095]\n\nCREATED:\n  - CODE-WT-0048\n  - SPEC-WT-0011\n  - TEST-WT-0024\n```\n\nEach artifact then records:\n\n```text\nartifact_id: CODE-WT-0048\ncreated_by: PySpc.260721.0095\nderived_from:\n  - EQ-WT-0023\n  - SPEC-WT-0011\nlineage:\n  - PySpc.260721.0095\n```\n\nThat lets an equation retain one stable identity through several rounds of correction.\n\n## Agent setup file\n\nEvery individual LLM instance gets one setup file. A provisional structure:\n\n```yaml\nagent:\n  id: PySpc\n  name: Python Specification Laboratory\n  platform: chatgpt-codex\n  setup_version: \"1.0\"\n  created: \"2026-07-21\"\n  status: active\n\nsignature:\n  timezone: America/New_York\n  sequence_mode: global_monotonic\n  next_sequence: 96\n  required_on_every_reply: true\n\nrole:\n  primary: Translate approved equation specifications into testable Python\n  secondary:\n    - Build self-diagnostic test matrices\n    - Produce execution instructions\n\nscope:\n  may:\n    - Read approved specifications\n    - Write Python\n    - Execute code if tools are available\n  may_not:\n    - Alter governing equations without escalation\n    - Promote hypotheses\n    - Interpret CODATA matches as validation\n    - Modify upstream lineage\n\nsources:\n  permitted:\n    - REG-EQUATIONS\n    - REG-SYMBOLS\n    - approved module packets\n  prohibited:\n    - unapproved speculative notes\n\noutputs:\n  required:\n    - implementation\n    - equation-to-code map\n    - test matrix\n    - expected failure behavior\n    - dependency list\n    - signatory packet\n\nhandoff:\n  preferred_next:\n    - EqC\n    - PyRun\n  full_lineage_required: true\n```\n\nThe setup version matters. Every signed reply must resolve to the setup version active at that sequence number.\n\nMinor rule changes can increment the setup version. A fundamental role or corpus change should usually produce a new agent identity, because it is no longer operationally the same laboratory.\n\nRetired IDs are never reused.\n\n## Individual identity versus reusable role\n\nWe need both.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nrole_id: PYTHON_SPECIFICATION\nagent_id: PySpc\n```\n\nIf another LLM instance is created with the same role:\n\n```text\nrole_id: PYTHON_SPECIFICATION\nagent_id: PySpB\n```\n\nThey share a role template but remain distinguishable individuals.\n\nThis matters because two instances with identical prompts but different conversation histories or source corpora are not actually interchangeable.\n\n## Capability registry\n\nCapabilities should not be inferred solely from the model brand. They belong to the specific configured instance.\n\nA useful vocabulary is:\n\n- `UNAVAILABLE`\n- `AVAILABLE_UNVERIFIED`\n- `CONDITIONAL`\n- `VERIFIED`\n- `REPEATEDLY_VERIFIED`\n- `UNRELIABLE`\n\nEach rating should include evidence.\n\nExample:\n\n```yaml\ncapabilities:\n  source_retrieval:\n    status: unavailable\n\n  python_generation:\n    status: repeatedly_verified\n    evidence:\n      - CAL-PY-0003\n      - CAL-PY-0008\n\n  python_execution:\n    status: verified\n    evidence:\n      - CAL-RUN-0012\n\n  lean_generation:\n    status: available_unverified\n\n  lean_execution:\n    status: unavailable\n\n  equation_to_code_trace:\n    status: verified\n    evidence:\n      - CAL-EQC-0005\n\n  citation_precision:\n    status: conditional\n    notes: Reliable only for attached local sources\n```\n\nThe supervisor routes a task by comparing its required capabilities against verified agent capabilities.\n\nExample task contract:\n\n```yaml\nrequires:\n  source_retrieval: verified\n  long_corpus_synthesis: verified\n  exact_citation: conditional_or_better\n  python_execution: not_required\n```\n\nThat task might go to the math NotebookLM, but not to `PySpc`.\n\n## Capability categories\n\nThe registry should cover at least these groups.\n\n### Source access\n\n- Access to NotebookLM corpus.\n- Access to project files.\n- Access to public web sources.\n- Citation retrieval.\n- Page/section precision.\n- Sequential document reading.\n- Large-corpus synthesis.\n- Ability to distinguish quoted material from generated interpretation.\n\n### Mathematical work\n\n- Equation extraction.\n- Symbol/type analysis.\n- Dimensional analysis.\n- Algebraic manipulation.\n- Differential geometry.\n- topology/knot theory.\n- PDE analysis.\n- numerical analysis.\n- standard-physics benchmarking.\n- SAT/H(s)H translation.\n- ability to return a null result.\n\n### Code\n\n- Static code reading.\n- Equation-to-code comparison.\n- Python generation.\n- Python execution.\n- test generation.\n- numerical diagnostics.\n- symbolic computation.\n- repository editing.\n- report generation.\n\n### Formal systems\n\n- Lean writing.\n- Lean execution.\n- theorem-library search.\n- dependency reconstruction.\n- assumption auditing.\n- formal counterexample construction.\n\n### Communication\n\n- Plaintext derivation modules.\n- YAML/JSON packets.\n- LaTeX.\n- diagrams.\n- executive summaries.\n- mainstream-physics translation.\n- citation formatting.\n\n### Operational limits\n\n- Context persistence.\n- corpus boundaries.\n- inability to execute.\n- inability to browse.\n- inability to inspect other agents.\n- tendency to merge conflicting sources.\n- susceptibility to target leakage.\n- known formatting or notation failures.\n\n## Provisional laboratory roster\n\nUsing your examples, the eventual roster might look something like this:\n\n| Signature | Function |\n|---|---|\n| `Sup` | Central supervisor and router |\n| `MxEq` | Mathematical source extraction |\n| `PySpc` | Python specification/implementation |\n| `EqC` | Pre-run equation-to-code QC |\n| `PyRun` | Code execution and run capture |\n| `Ev` | Numerical/model evaluation |\n| `LnChk` | Lean derivation formalization/checking |\n| `SrcV` | Source and citation verification |\n| `BkInt` | Mathematical backbone integration |\n| `LaTx` | Plaintext-to-LaTeX derivation rendering |\n\nThese are only candidate signatures. Each would need a distinctive human name, unique abbreviation, setup file, and capability record.\n\n## Chain integrity\n\nThe bracket chain is excellent provenance, but it is not cryptographic. An LLM could accidentally omit or alter a segment.\n\nFor repository artifacts, each signatory packet should also record:\n\n- input artifact hash;\n- output artifact hash;\n- parent response;\n- active setup version;\n- repository commit when applicable.\n\nThen the human-readable chain answers “who handled this?” while hashes and repository history answer “is this the same artifact?”\n\n## What this adds to the supervisory skill\n\nThe eventual skill will need rules for:\n\n- assigning and registering individual LLM identities;\n- generating setup files from role templates;\n- enforcing reply signatures;\n- validating sequence numbers;\n- constructing and appending lineages;\n- handling branches and merges;\n- preventing agents from rewriting upstream chains;\n- attaching actions and dispositions to signatures;\n- routing by verified capability;\n- recording calibration evidence;\n- distinguishing agent replies from stable artifacts;\n- checking that the current agent stayed within its assigned role.\n\nThe next pre-build design step should be the capability vocabulary and agent setup schema together. Once those are stable, we can define the initial roster and route one real mathematical-extraction task through it on paper before encoding the skill."
